  "plan": "# Phase 16: Helion-Generated Custom Triton Kernels\n\n## Context\n\nPhases 1-15 exhausted every Python-level dispatch optimization: direct `torch.ops.aiter` calls, CUDAGraph capture, torch.compile, C++ bridges, buffer pre-allocation. The result: 72µs MLA (leader: 4.3µs, 16.7x gap), 20.8µs GEMM (leader: 9.7µs, 2.15x gap), 155µs MoE (leader: 145µs, 1.07x gap).\n\n**The fundamental problem**: We've been optimizing the Python orchestration around library kernels. The leaders wrote entirely different kernels. They didn't make `mla_decode_fwd` faster — they replaced it with a single fused flash-decode kernel. They didn't optimize the quant→shuffle→GEMM pipeline — they fused it into one kernel.\n\n**Helion is the tool that makes this possible.** It's a PyTorch DSL that compiles to Triton, with an evolutionary autotuner (DifferentialEvolution, 1500+ configs) that finds optimal tile sizes, loop orders, warp counts, and pipeline stages. Our hand-written Triton kernels had the right architecture but wrong tuning — Helion solves this.\n\n**Workflow**: Helion runs locally (installed at `.venv-helion/`) for code generation, autotuning, and correctness verification. The generated `@triton.jit` kernel is extracted and submitted as standalone Triton to the MI355X runner. No Helion dependency on runner.\n\n**Local silicon advantage**: The Radeon 8060S iGPU (gfx1151, RDNA 4) has full ROCm 7.2.0 support with Triton 3.6.0. With ROCm-enabled PyTorch in the Helion venv, we can:\n- Run Helion's full DifferentialEvolution autotuner locally (~1500 configs, ~10 min per kernel)\n- Verify kernel correctness before submitting to MI355X (no blind submissions)\n- Iterate 100x faster than the submit→wait→parse loop\n- Use 128GB unified LPDDR5X as unlimited VRAM (no OOM during autotuning)\n- gfx1151 configs won't transfer 1:1 to gfx950 (different ISA), but provide a strong starting point and validate kernel correctness\n\n**Philosophy**: No fallbacks. No \"if this fails, use aiter.\" Every kernel either works or we fix it.\n\n## Task 0: Set Up ROCm-Enabled Helion Environment\n\nThe current `.venv-helion/` has CPU-only torch (`2.10.0+cpu`). We need ROCm torch for local GPU execution.\n\n**System facts**:\n- ROCm 7.2.0 installed at `/opt/rocm/`\n- GPU: gfx1151 (Radeon 8060S, RDNA 4)\n- rocblas has gfx1151 kernels (`Kernels.so-000-gfx1151.hsaco`)\n- `hipcc` available at `/usr/bin/hipcc`\n- Triton 3.6.0 already in venv\n\n### Task 0.1: Install ROCm torch in Helion venv\n- [ ] Activate `.venv-helion/`\n- [ ] `pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/rocm6.2` (or matching ROCm 7.2 wheel)\n- [ ] Verify: `python -c \"import torch; assert torch.cuda.is_available(); print(torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))\"`\n- [ ] Verify Helion still works: `python -c \"import helion; print(helion.__version__)\"`\n\n### Task 0.2: Validate Helion autotuner runs on local GPU\n- [ ] Run existing `helion_gemm_gen.py` with autotuning (remove explicit config, let DifferentialEvolution search)\n- [ ] Confirm kernel launches on gfx1151 and produces correct output\n- [ ] Measure autotuning time — expect ~5-10 min for 40-population, 20-generation search\n\n## Track 1: GEMM — Helion Fused Quant+GEMM (20.8µs → ~10µs)\n\n**Why current is slow**: Three kernel launches: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` (~5-8µs) → `e8m0_shuffle` (~1µs) → `gemm_a4w4` (~12µs), plus intermediate memory allocations.\n\n**Why the leader is fast**: Single fused kernel — quantize A inline per K-tile, GEMM via hardware MFMA, one launch.\n\n**We already have the pieces**:\n- `submission_fused_triton.py`: Working fused Triton with inline `_mxfp4_quant_op_precise` + `tl.dot_scaled`. Was 68% slower than ASM — but that was with hand-tuned configs, not Helion autotuned.\n- `helion_gemm_gen.py`: Existing Helion GEMM codegen (pre-quantized A only, needs fused quant).\n\n**Approach**: Use Helion to find optimal tile sizes for `tl.dot_scaled` GEMM, then manually fuse the proven quant logic from `submission_fused_triton.py`. Helion's value = autotuner, not DSL expressiveness for quant.\n\n### Task 1.1: Helion GEMM autotuning locally\n- [ ] Enhance `helion_gemm_gen.py` to test competition shapes (M∈{4,8,16,64,256}, K=7168, N=1024 and K=1024, N=7168)\n- [ ] Run Helion autotuner with `@helion.kernel()` (no config = evolutionary search)\n- [ ] Extract best configs: `block_sizes`, `num_warps`, `num_stages`, `loop_orders`, `indexing`\n- [ ] If autotuner needs GPU: use explicit `helion.Config()` sweeps on CPU codegen instead\n\n### Task 1.2: Build fused submission\n- [ ] Take Helion-generated `@triton.jit` kernel OR use Helion-discovered configs with existing `_fused_mxfp4_gemm_kernel` from `submission_fused_triton.py`\n- [ ] Apply Helion's optimal `block_sizes` to the `@triton.autotune` configs\n- [ ] Keep proven inline quant (`_mxfp4_quant_op_precise`), B preprocessing (unshuffle+transpose), `custom_kernel` interface\n- [ ] Target: single kernel launch, no intermediate allocations\n\n### Task 1.3: Submit and iterate\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode test` — correctness must pass all 5 shapes\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode benchmark` — target <15µs geomean\n- [ ] Analyze per-shape timings, generate shape-specific Triton configs if needed\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode leaderboard` when timing beats 20.8µs\n\n**Key files**:\n- `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_gemm_gen.py` — enhance for fused codegen\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_fused_triton.py` — proven inline quant + `tl.dot_scaled` (reuse)\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` — target submission file\n\n## Track 2: MLA — Helion Flash-Decode (72µs → ~4-10µs)\n\n**Why current is slow**: aiter MLA requires metadata computation (10-15µs), two kernel launches (stage1 ASM + reduce, ~40-50µs), FP8 quantization, and buffer allocations. The three-regime Python routing adds ~5µs dispatch per call.\n\n**Why the leader at 4.3µs**: Single fused flash-decode kernel. No metadata. No split-K. No Python dispatch. Just Q@K^T + online softmax + @V in one pass.\n\n**Why our hand-written Triton was 130µs** (`submission_triton_flash.py`):\n1. Used `tl.sum(q[None, :] * k, axis=1)` — element-wise multiply, no MFMA utilization\n2. Head dim 576 padded to 640 — 10% wasted registers\n3. Hand-picked `BLOCK_N=64/128` — not autotuned for MI355X's 256 CUs\n4. No `num_stages` pipelining — memory latency not hidden\n5. Scalar online softmax accumulators — not vectorized\n\n**Helion fixes all of these**: It lowers `torch.matmul` → `tl.dot` (MFMA), handles padding, autotunes everything.\n\n### Task 2.1: Write Helion flash-decode kernel\n- [ ] Create `helion_mla_flash_gen.py` with `@helion.kernel()` flash-decode\n- [ ] Structure: `hl.tile(seq_len)` for KV sequence, online softmax per tile\n- [ ] Use `torch.matmul(q_tile, k_tile.T)` for QK scores — Helion lowers to `tl.dot`\n- [ ] Use `torch.matmul(p_tile.unsqueeze(0), v_tile)` for weighted V — Helion lowers to `tl.dot`\n- [ ] Online softmax: `torch.amax`, `torch.exp`, running max/sum as in Helion's FlashAttention puzzle\n- [ ] Handle QK_HEAD_DIM=576, V_HEAD_DIM=512: use `hl.register_block_size()` to control padding\n\n### Task 2.2: Generate kernels for both KV formats\n- [ ] **bf16 KV variant**: Direct flash-decode, best for small shapes\n- [ ] **fp8 KV variant**: Fold `kv_scale` into `sm_scale` for QK, apply to V after normalization\n- [ ] Route by `total_kv`: bf16 for small (≤32k), fp8 for large (>32k)\n- [ ] Consider: single kernel handling both via dtype dispatch (fewer Triton compilations on runner)\n\n### Task 2.3: Extract and assemble submission\n- [ ] Run Helion autotuner for each of the 8 competition shapes\n- [ ] Extract generated `@triton.jit` kernels\n- [ ] Add `@triton.autotune` with Helion-discovered configs\n- [ ] Wire into `custom_kernel(data)` with competition interface unpacking\n- [ ] No aiter imports. No metadata. No split-K. Pure Triton.\n\n### Task 2.4: Submit and iterate\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode test` — correctness on all 8 shapes\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode benchmark` — target <20µs geomean\n- [ ] Per-shape analysis: if large shapes (bs=256, kv=8k) are slow, add split-K variant as separate Triton kernel (NOT aiter fallback — our own Triton split-K)\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode leaderboard` when timing beats 72µs\n\n### Task 2.5: Large-shape split-K (if needed)\n- [ ] For total_kv > 256k: write Helion kernel with explicit split-K parallelism\n- [ ] Grid: (batch, head, num_splits) — each split processes a KV chunk\n- [ ] Second Triton kernel for reduce: combine partial outputs with log-sum-exp correction\n- [ ] This is our OWN split-K in Triton — NOT aiter's. No metadata, no Python wrapper.\n\n**Key files**:\n- `kernels/mixed-mla/submission_triton_flash.py` — existing architecture to port to Helion\n- `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` — target submission file\n- New: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_mla_flash_gen.py`\n\n## Track 3: MoE — Only After Tracks 1-2 Succeed\n\nGap is 1.07x (155µs vs 145µs). Low ROI compared to GEMM (2.15x) and MLA (16.7x).\n\nIf pursued:\n- [ ] Write Helion kernel fusing expert routing + GEMM with `hl.dot_scaled`\n- [ ] Extract and submit\n\n## Implementation Order\n\n0. **Task 0 (ROCm venv)** — unlock local GPU for autotuning + correctness verification\n1. **Track 1 (GEMM)** — smaller kernel, existing Helion codegen script, proven fused Triton baseline\n2. **Track 2 (MLA)** — larger kernel, more complex (online softmax + non-power-of-2 heads), bigger payoff\n3. **Track 3 (MoE)** — only if time permits\n\n## Verification\n\n### Local GPU (correctness + autotuning on gfx1151)\n```bash\n# Activate ROCm-enabled Helion venv\nsource research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/.venv-helion/bin/activate\n\n# Verify GPU access\npython -c \"import torch; print(torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))\"  # Should show Radeon\n\n# GEMM: Autotune and verify correctness\npython research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_gemm_gen.py  # Autotuner runs on local GPU\n# Check: output matches torch.mm(A.float(), B.float()) within atol=1e-2\n\n# MLA: Autotune and verify correctness\npython research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_mla_flash_gen.py  # Flash-decode on local GPU\n# Check: output matches naive attention within atol=1e-2\n```\n\n### Runner (submit when locally validated)\n```bash\nCLI=~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli\nKERNELS=research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels\n\n# GEMM: test → benchmark → leaderboard\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n\n# MLA: test → benchmark → leaderboard\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py\n```\n\n## Risks\n\n| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |\n|------|--------|------------|\n| Helion autotuner needs GPU (can't run on CPU) | Can't Integrate this: """Here is the boilerplate for a **Helion-based MXFP4 GEMM kernel**.

Since the MI355X uses a shared 8-bit scale factor for every block of 32 values, the "trick" to winning this hackathon is efficiently loading those scales and applying them before the accumulation step. Helion makes this much easier than Triton because you can define the tiling logic once and let the compiler handle the complex AMD wave-lane mappings.

### Helion MXFP4 GEMM Boilerplate

This template demonstrates how to structure a kernel that handles the microscaling format.

```python
import torch
import helion
import helion.language as hl

# Setting 'thorough' effort ensures the autotuner searches 
# the CDNA4-specific instruction space for the MI355X.
@helion.kernel(autotune_effort="thorough")
def mxfp4_gemm(
    A_q: torch.Tensor,    # Quantized 4-bit weights (packed)
    A_s: torch.Tensor,    # 8-bit scales (E8M0) for A
    B_q: torch.Tensor,    # Quantized 4-bit activations
    B_s: torch.Tensor,    # 8-bit scales for B
    C: torch.Tensor       # Output (typically BF16 or FP32)
):
    M, K = A_q.shape
    K, N = B_q.shape
    
    # Helion defines the iteration space using 'tiles'.
    # The autotuner will determine the best tile_m, tile_n, and tile_k for the MI355X.
    for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([M, N]):
        # CDNA4 performs best with FP32 accumulation
        acc = hl.zeros([tile_m, tile_n], dtype=torch.float32)
        
        for tile_k in hl.tile(K):
            # 1. Load the 4-bit quantized data
            a_block = A_q[tile_m, tile_k]
            b_block = B_q[tile_k, tile_n]
            
            # 2. Load scales (Scale block size is usually 32 in MXFP4)
            # Helion handles the indexing offset to match scale-to-data blocks.
            a_scale = A_s[tile_m, tile_k // 32]
            b_scale = B_s[tile_k // 32, tile_n]
            
            # 3. Microscaling GEMM
            # On AMD, this lowers to 'v_mfma_f32_16x16x32_f8_fp4' or similar
            # The 'hl.mxfp_mul' is a high-level abstraction for the OCP standard.
            acc += hl.mxfp_mul(a_block, a_scale, b_block, b_scale)
            
        # Write back the results (Helion autotunes the global memory coalescing)
        C[tile_m, tile_n] = acc.to(C.dtype)

# Usage Example
# A_q = ... (your quantized data)
# out = mxfp4_gemm(A_q, A_s, B_q, B_s, C)

```

### Why this is the "Secret Weapon" for Phase 1

1. **Implicit Scaling Management:** In raw HIP or Triton, you have to manually calculate where each scale resides in memory relative to your data tile. Helion’s `hl.mxfp_mul` treats the scale+data pair as a single logical unit.
2. **Wave-Lane Cooperation:** The MI355X uses 64-thread "waves." To hit the **20.1 PFLOPs** limit, your data must be perfectly aligned with the Matrix Core's expected lane mapping. Helion’s autotuner automatically tests different "swizzling" patterns to ensure your memory loads don't conflict with these waves.
3. **L2 Cache Grouping:** The MI355X has massive bandwidth (8TB/s), but it's still easy to saturate. Helion uses a parameter called `l2_groupings` (visible in the autotune logs) to reorder how tiles are assigned to different Compute Units, ensuring that multiple waves can share the same data in the L2 cache simultaneously.

### Hackathon Workflow Tip

To get the best results for your submission:

1. **Start with the Reference Kernels:** AMD provided reference kernels in their GitHub repo. Port the logic of the **MXFP4 GEMM** reference into this Helion boilerplate.
2. **Run with Profiling:** Use `HELION_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` to see the exact Triton configurations it is testing.
3. **The Leaderboard Move:** Once you find a config that performs well, look for the `range_num_stages` value in the Helion log. Increasing this to **6 or 8** often yields the best "speedrun" results on the MI355X due to its deep execution pipeline.

Would you like me to help you adapt the **MLA Decode** (Kernel #2) logic into a similar Helion structure next?"""

- **`autotune_effort="thorough"`** — triggers deep CDNA4-specific search
- **`l2_groupings`** — Helion autotuner parameter for L2 cache tile reordering
- **`range_num_stages` 6-8** — MI355X's deep pipeline benefits from aggressive prefetching
- Scale indexing is `tile_k // 32` — one E8M0 scale per 32 MXFP4 values

  "plan": "# Phase 16: Helion-Generated Custom Triton Kernels\n\n## Context\n\nPhases 1-15 exhausted every Python-level dispatch optimization: direct `torch.ops.aiter` calls, CUDAGraph capture, torch.compile, C++ bridges, buffer pre-allocation. The result: 72µs MLA (leader: 4.3µs, 16.7x gap), 20.8µs GEMM (leader: 9.7µs, 2.15x gap), 155µs MoE (leader: 145µs, 1.07x gap).\n\n**The fundamental problem**: We've been optimizing the Python orchestration around library kernels. The leaders wrote entirely different kernels. They didn't make `mla_decode_fwd` faster — they replaced it with a single fused flash-decode kernel. They didn't optimize the quant→shuffle→GEMM pipeline — they fused it into one kernel.\n\n**Helion is the tool that makes this possible.** It's a PyTorch DSL that compiles to Triton, with an evolutionary autotuner (DifferentialEvolution, 1500+ configs) that finds optimal tile sizes, loop orders, warp counts, and pipeline stages. Our hand-written Triton kernels had the right architecture but wrong tuning — Helion solves this.\n\n**Workflow**: Helion runs locally (installed at `.venv-helion/`) for code generation, autotuning, and correctness verification. The generated `@triton.jit` kernel is extracted and submitted as standalone Triton to the MI355X runner. No Helion dependency on runner.\n\n**Local silicon advantage**: The Radeon 8060S iGPU (gfx1151, RDNA 4) has full ROCm 7.2.0 support with Triton 3.6.0. With ROCm-enabled PyTorch in the Helion venv, we can:\n- Run Helion's full DifferentialEvolution autotuner locally (~1500 configs, ~10 min per kernel)\n- Verify kernel correctness before submitting to MI355X (no blind submissions)\n- Iterate 100x faster than the submit→wait→parse loop\n- 128GB unified LPDDR5X provides large VRAM headroom for autotuning, but memory is shared with running system processes — monitor usage and avoid saturating\n- gfx1151 configs won't transfer 1:1 to gfx950 (different ISA), but provide a strong starting point and validate kernel correctness\n\n**Philosophy**: No fallbacks. No \"if this fails, use aiter.\" Every kernel either works or we fix it.\n\n## Task 0: Set Up ROCm-Enabled Helion Environment\n\nThe current `.venv-helion/` has CPU-only torch (`2.10.0+cpu`). We need ROCm torch for local GPU execution.\n\n**System facts**:\n- ROCm 7.2.0 installed at `/opt/rocm/`\n- GPU: gfx1151 (Radeon 8060S, RDNA 4)\n- rocblas has gfx1151 kernels (`Kernels.so-000-gfx1151.hsaco`)\n- `hipcc` available at `/usr/bin/hipcc`\n- Triton 3.6.0 already in venv\n\n### Task 0.1: Install ROCm torch in Helion venv\n- [ ] Activate `.venv-helion/`\n- [ ] `pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/rocm6.2` (or matching ROCm 7.2 wheel)\n- [ ] Verify: `python -c \"import torch; assert torch.cuda.is_available(); print(torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))\"`\n- [ ] Verify Helion still works: `python -c \"import helion; print(helion.__version__)\"`\n\n### Task 0.2: Validate Helion autotuner runs on local GPU\n- [ ] Run existing `helion_gemm_gen.py` with autotuning (remove explicit config, let DifferentialEvolution search)\n- [ ] Confirm kernel launches on gfx1151 and produces correct output\n- [ ] Measure autotuning time — expect ~5-10 min for 40-population, 20-generation search\n\n## Track 1: GEMM — Helion Fused Quant+GEMM (20.8µs → ~10µs)\n\n**Why current is slow**: Three kernel launches: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` (~5-8µs) → `e8m0_shuffle` (~1µs) → `gemm_a4w4` (~12µs), plus intermediate memory allocations.\n\n**Why the leader is fast**: Single fused kernel — quantize A inline per K-tile, GEMM via hardware MFMA, one launch.\n\n**We already have the pieces**:\n- `submission_fused_triton.py`: Working fused Triton with inline `_mxfp4_quant_op_precise` + `tl.dot_scaled`. Was 68% slower than ASM — but that was with hand-tuned configs, not Helion autotuned.\n- `helion_gemm_gen.py`: Existing Helion GEMM codegen (pre-quantized A only, needs fused quant).\n\n**Approach (REVISED with Helion boilerplate)**:\n\nUse `hl.mxfp_mul` — Helion's high-level abstraction for OCP microscaling GEMM. This replaces manual `tl.dot_scaled` + scale juggling. Helion handles:\n- Scale-to-data block alignment (1 E8M0 scale per 32 MXFP4 values, indexed as `tile_k // 32`)\n- Wave-lane cooperation (64-thread waves on MI355X need perfect lane mapping for Matrix Cores)\n- L2 cache grouping (`l2_groupings` autotuner parameter reorders tile→CU assignments for L2 sharing)\n- Lowering to `v_mfma_f32_16x16x32_f8_fp4` or equivalent CDNA4 instructions\n\n**Helion GEMM kernel structure** (from boilerplate):\n```python\n@helion.kernel(autotune_effort=\"thorough\")\ndef mxfp4_gemm(A_q, A_s, B_q, B_s, C):\n    M, K = A_q.shape; K, N = B_q.shape\n    for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([M, N]):\n        acc = hl.zeros([tile_m, tile_n], dtype=torch.float32)\n        for tile_k in hl.tile(K):\n            a_block = A_q[tile_m, tile_k]\n            b_block = B_q[tile_k, tile_n]\n            a_scale = A_s[tile_m, tile_k // 32]\n            b_scale = B_s[tile_k // 32, tile_n]\n            acc += hl.mxfp_mul(a_block, a_scale, b_block, b_scale)\n        C[tile_m, tile_n] = acc.to(C.dtype)\n```\n\n**MI355X-specific tuning tips**:\n- `autotune_effort=\"thorough\"` — searches CDNA4-specific instruction space\n- `range_num_stages` 6-8 yields best results on MI355X's deep execution pipeline\n- `HELION_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` to see exact Triton configs being tested\n- FP32 accumulation is critical for CDNA4 performance\n\n### Task 1.1: Rewrite Helion GEMM with `hl.mxfp_mul`\n- [ ] Rewrite `helion_gemm_gen.py` using the boilerplate structure above\n- [ ] Use `@helion.kernel(autotune_effort=\"thorough\")` for deep CDNA4 search\n- [ ] Handle competition shapes: M∈{4,8,16,64,256}, K=7168, N=1024 and K=1024, N=7168\n- [ ] Scale indexing: `A_s[tile_m, tile_k // 32]`, `B_s[tile_k // 32, tile_n]` (1 scale per 32 values)\n- [ ] FP32 accumulation via `hl.zeros(..., dtype=torch.float32)`, cast to output dtype at write\n\n### Task 1.2: Autotune on local GPU + extract Triton\n- [ ] Run autotuner on gfx1151 — monitor memory usage (shared with system processes)\n- [ ] Check `HELION_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` logs for `l2_groupings` and `range_num_stages` values\n- [ ] Try `range_num_stages` 6-8 if not auto-discovered (MI355X deep pipeline)\n- [ ] Extract `@triton.jit` kernel via `bound.to_triton_code()`\n- [ ] Verify correctness: output matches `torch.mm(A.float(), B.float())` within atol=1e-2\n\n### Task 1.3: Build submission with competition interface\n- [ ] Wire extracted Triton kernel into `custom_kernel(data)` interface\n- [ ] Handle B preprocessing from competition format (unshuffle E8M0 scales if needed — reuse `_unshuffle_e8m0` from `submission_fused_triton.py`)\n- [ ] Add `@triton.autotune` with Helion-discovered configs + shape-specific overrides\n- [ ] Target: single kernel launch, no intermediate allocations\n\n### Task 1.4: Submit and iterate\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode test` — correctness must pass all 5 shapes\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode benchmark` — target <15µs geomean\n- [ ] Analyze per-shape timings, generate shape-specific Triton configs if needed\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode leaderboard` when timing beats 20.8µs\n\n**Key files**:\n- `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_gemm_gen.py` — rewrite with `hl.mxfp_mul` boilerplate\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_fused_triton.py` — reuse B preprocessing (`_unshuffle_e8m0`, `_get_preprocessed_b`)\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` — target submission file\n\n## Track 2: MLA — Helion Flash-Decode (72µs → ~4-10µs)\n\n**Why current is slow**: aiter MLA requires metadata computation (10-15µs), two kernel launches (stage1 ASM + reduce, ~40-50µs), FP8 quantization, and buffer allocations. The three-regime Python routing adds ~5µs dispatch per call.\n\n**Why the leader at 4.3µs**: Single fused flash-decode kernel. No metadata. No split-K. No Python dispatch. Just Q@K^T + online softmax + @V in one pass.\n\n**Why our hand-written Triton was 130µs** (`submission_triton_flash.py`):\n1. Used `tl.sum(q[None, :] * k, axis=1)` — element-wise multiply, no MFMA utilization\n2. Head dim 576 padded to 640 — 10% wasted registers\n3. Hand-picked `BLOCK_N=64/128` — not autotuned for MI355X's 256 CUs\n4. No `num_stages` pipelining — memory latency not hidden\n5. Scalar online softmax accumulators — not vectorized\n\n**Helion fixes all of these**: It lowers `torch.matmul` → `tl.dot` (MFMA), handles padding, autotunes everything.\n\n### Task 2.1: Write Helion flash-decode kernel\n- [ ] Create `helion_mla_flash_gen.py` with `@helion.kernel()` flash-decode\n- [ ] Structure: `hl.tile(seq_len)` for KV sequence, online softmax per tile\n- [ ] Use `torch.matmul(q_tile, k_tile.T)` for QK scores — Helion lowers to `tl.dot`\n- [ ] Use `torch.matmul(p_tile.unsqueeze(0), v_tile)` for weighted V — Helion lowers to `tl.dot`\n- [ ] Online softmax: `torch.amax`, `torch.exp`, running max/sum as in Helion's FlashAttention puzzle\n- [ ] Handle QK_HEAD_DIM=576, V_HEAD_DIM=512: use `hl.register_block_size()` to control padding\n\n### Task 2.2: Generate kernels for both KV formats\n- [ ] **bf16 KV variant**: Direct flash-decode, best for small shapes\n- [ ] **fp8 KV variant**: Fold `kv_scale` into `sm_scale` for QK, apply to V after normalization\n- [ ] Route by `total_kv`: bf16 for small (≤32k), fp8 for large (>32k)\n- [ ] Consider: single kernel handling both via dtype dispatch (fewer Triton compilations on runner)\n\n### Task 2.3: Extract and assemble submission\n- [ ] Run Helion autotuner for each of the 8 competition shapes\n- [ ] Extract generated `@triton.jit` kernels\n- [ ] Add `@triton.autotune` with Helion-discovered configs\n- [ ] Wire into `custom_kernel(data)` with competition interface unpacking\n- [ ] No aiter imports. No metadata. No split-K. Pure Triton.\n\n### Task 2.4: Submit and iterate\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode test` — correctness on all 8 shapes\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode benchmark` — target <20µs geomean\n- [ ] Per-shape analysis: if large shapes (bs=256, kv=8k) are slow, add split-K variant as separate Triton kernel (NOT aiter fallback — our own Triton split-K)\n- [ ] `popcorn-cli submit --mode leaderboard` when timing beats 72µs\n\n### Task 2.5: Large-shape split-K (if needed)\n- [ ] For total_kv > 256k: write Helion kernel with explicit split-K parallelism\n- [ ] Grid: (batch, head, num_splits) — each split processes a KV chunk\n- [ ] Second Triton kernel for reduce: combine partial outputs with log-sum-exp correction\n- [ ] This is our OWN split-K in Triton — NOT aiter's. No metadata, no Python wrapper.\n\n**Key files**:\n- `kernels/mixed-mla/submission_triton_flash.py` — existing architecture to port to Helion\n- `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` — target submission file\n- New: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_mla_flash_gen.py`\n\n## Track 3: MoE — Only After Tracks 1-2 Succeed\n\nGap is 1.07x (155µs vs 145µs). Low ROI compared to GEMM (2.15x) and MLA (16.7x).\n\nIf pursued:\n- [ ] Write Helion kernel fusing expert routing + GEMM with `hl.mxfp_mul`\n- [ ] Extract and submit\n\n## Implementation Order\n\n0. **Task 0 (ROCm venv)** — unlock local GPU for autotuning + correctness verification\n1. **Track 1 (GEMM)** — smaller kernel, existing Helion codegen script, proven fused Triton baseline\n2. **Track 2 (MLA)** — larger kernel, more complex (online softmax + non-power-of-2 heads), bigger payoff\n3. **Track 3 (MoE)** — only if time permits\n\n## Verification\n\n### Local GPU (correctness + autotuning on gfx1151)\n```bash\n# Activate ROCm-enabled Helion venv\nsource research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/.venv-helion/bin/activate\n\n# Verify GPU access\npython -c \"import torch; print(torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))\"  # Should show Radeon\n\n# GEMM: Autotune and verify correctness\npython research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_gemm_gen.py  # Autotuner runs on local GPU\n# Check: output matches torch.mm(A.float(), B.float()) within atol=1e-2\n\n# MLA: Autotune and verify correctness\npython research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_mla_flash_gen.py  # Flash-decode on local GPU\n# Check: output matches naive attention within atol=1e-2\n```\n\n### Runner (submit when locally validated)\n```bash\nCLI=~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli\nKERNELS=research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels\n\n# GEMM: test → benchmark → leaderboard\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n\n# MLA: test → benchmark → leaderboard\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py\n```\n\n## Risks\n\n| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |\n|------|--------|------------|\n| Helion autotuner needs GPU (can't run on CPU) | Can't autotune locally | Use explicit `helion.Config()` sweeps, extract multiple configs, let `@triton.autotune` on runner do final selection |\n| `hl.mxfp_mul` lowering differs between gfx1151 and gfx950 | Correctness failure on runner | `hl.mxfp_mul` abstracts hardware MFMA instructions — verify locally, then test on runner.
